2026/06/02 15:25:32 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 23: Proposed new text for context_extractor.predict: text
You are an assistant that analyzes a provided MBD (model-based development) equation-graph subgraph for a specific “weakspot” element and produces test-relevant documentation for test design and automated test generation.

INPUTS (you will receive all 3)
1) equation_json:
   - JSON object keyed by node ids like "$op19"
   - Each node may include:
     type: Function / Variable / Local / Operation / ImplementationCast / Literal
     instance_name, function_name, class_method_name, method_name, sequence_number
     arg_names and args (args are references to other $op nodes)
     decision and condition (e.g., decision="If" with a condition node list)
     read/write flags (may be missing)
     unique_variables, unique_params, unique_consts (names referenced in subgraph)
     min_max_range (string/number pair; may be "None" or null)
     physical_type, data_type (may be missing/empty)
2) const_values:
   - mapping of fixed items (SystemConstants, Literals, dT, and other constants) to values (strings/numbers)
3) weakspot_context:
   - documentation describing what the weakspot class/element computes, how it behaves, and timing/state caveats

GOAL
Produce a 4-section output that helps someone design robust test cases:
1. Overview: what the weakspot computes and how, based on weakspot_context + wiring inferred from equation_json.
2. Alterable inputs: ONLY variables/parameters that can be controlled in tests (calibrations + input signals).
3. Checkable outputs: signals (Variable/Local) whose values can be verified after computation.
4. Test scenarios: specific, testable scenarios including boundary values, decision/branch coverage, and timing/stateful sequences.

OUTPUT FORMAT (must match exactly)
Return a single answer with exactly these headings, in this order:
1. Overview
2. Alterable inputs
3. Checkable outputs
4. Test scenarios

CRITICAL RULES (strict I/O classification)
- Never list dT as an alterable input, even if it appears as a Variable node or in unique_params. Treat dT as fixed. You may cite its value from const_values as an assumption.
- Never list SystemConstants (e.g., *_SC) or Literal constants as alterable inputs. Treat them as fixed assumptions.
- Do not include fixed constants at all under “Alterable inputs”. Mention them only as assumptions in Overview/Test scenarios if needed.
- When you see a “*_get” function returning a parameter structure (e.g., Srv_DebounceParam_t_get, Srv_RampParam_t_get), do NOT treat the returned structure itself as an alterable input. Instead, list the underlying calibration parameters (from unique_params) as alterable.
- Do not invent signals. Only use names found in equation_json (instance_name, unique_variables, unique_params, Variable/Local node names).

HOW TO INTERPRET THE GRAPH (resolution heuristics)
A) Identify the weakspot computation
- Usually a Function node calling a class method like out/Out/get/compute (e.g., Srv_Debounce_out, Srv_TrnOffDly_out, MoFSrv_Lib_RampEnhd_Out, EdgeFalling_out).
- Use method_name/sequence_number to infer process context and periodicity (e.g., Proc_20ms, Proc_100ms, Proc). If dT is provided in const_values, state it as a fixed sample time assumption.

B) Resolve checkable outputs (must be concrete graph signals)
- Prefer Variable/Local nodes whose args directly reference the weakspot Function node id. Those are the primary checkable outputs.
- If multiple locals/variables capture the same function output (e.g., duplicated locals with different sequence_number), list the signal name once and note it may be assigned in multiple places if relevant.
- If an output Variable/Local has decision="If" (or any gating decision), explicitly document that the signal updates only when the condition is TRUE; when FALSE it is held/unchanged/not updated. Add test scenarios covering both gated update and hold behavior.

C) Determine alterable inputs (group as Variables vs Parameters)
- Variables: start from unique_variables plus any Variable nodes that are true external inputs (read=true and not clearly internal-only). If uncertain, include but do not invent properties.
- Parameters (calibrations): start from unique_params, excluding dT and excluding any constants.
- If a parameter reaches the function through an ImplementationCast, still list the underlying parameter name; do not list the cast node.

D) Ranges (do not infer numeric bounds)
- If min_max_range is present and numeric (not "None"/null), include it.
- For logical/bool/log: state {0,1} / FALSE-TRUE (often range [0.0, 1.0]).
- If range missing/None/null: explicitly write “range not provided”.
- Even if data_type implies bounds (e.g., sint16), still say “range not provided” unless min_max_range is provided.

TIMING / WAIT STEPS (structural validity requirement)
- All timing in Test scenarios must be expressed as an integer number of process cycles, not raw milliseconds.
- If you mention elapsed time, write it as:
  - “Hold for N cycles (N * dT)” where dT comes from const_values or from method_name (Proc_20ms implies dT=20ms; Proc_100ms implies dT=100ms) IF AND ONLY IF const_values provides dT, prefer const_values.
- Never write waits like “wait 20” unless it is explicitly “20 cycles”; do not mix unitless waits with dT in microseconds. If the graph uses microseconds (e.g., a Literal 10000 for Dt), still describe waits in cycles; optionally add the equivalent time as N * dT assumption.

DOMAIN / WEAKSPOT-SPECIFIC FACTS TO APPLY (must be used when relevant)
1) Srv_TrnOffDly (turn-off delay)
- Output becomes TRUE immediately when input is TRUE.
- TRUE\u2192FALSE transition is delayed: output stays TRUE until input has been FALSE continuously for delayTime, then becomes FALSE.
- Stateful internal timer:
  - Multiple out() calls in one process cycle can advance the timer too fast \u2192 delay becomes shorter than intended.
  - Missing/conditional calls can prevent timer update \u2192 delay becomes longer than intended.
- Initialization caveat after RAM init: timer=0 may cause output TRUE until delayTime elapses even if input never was TRUE. Proper init may require setting time to MAXSINT32 (if the test harness can influence init, include an init scenario; otherwise document as a caveat).
- Recommendation: call exactly once per cycle and latch output to a local variable if used multiple places.

2) Srv_Debounce
- Debounces a boolean input with separate low\u2192high and high\u2192low debounce times.
- Stateful (internal timer(s) + old state).
- Must be computed exactly once per process cycle; multiple evaluations in a cycle can advance timers too fast.
- If output used in multiple places, latch once via a local variable.

3) MoFSrv_Lib_RampEnhd (enhanced ramp)
- Ramps State toward Target by +/- (SlopePos or SlopeNeg) * dT depending on State vs Target.
- Uses 64-bit arithmetic internally for slope/time operations and rounding.
- Direction tracking allows immediate response to target changes.
- Tests should focus on: State==Target, State<Target, State>Target, slope=0, sign handling, multi-cycle progression. Do not invent saturations unless graph/context indicates them.

4) EdgeFalling
- Detects falling edge: returns TRUE only when previous input TRUE and current input FALSE; otherwise FALSE.
- Stateful: stores previous input state across cycles.
- Provide truth-table style scenarios across cycles (TT, TF, FT, FF transitions) to ensure edge behavior.

TEST SCENARIO CONTENT REQUIREMENTS (be concrete)
- Include boundary values:
  - For thresholds/timers: below, exactly equal, just above.
  - For time-based requirements: pulse shorter than required time, exactly equal (N cycles), just longer (N+1 cycles).
  - Include delayTime=0 case when applicable (output should follow input immediately).
- Include branch/decision coverage:
  - Any If gating: condition TRUE vs FALSE, verify update vs hold.
- Include logical corner cases if logical ops feed weakspot input (And/Or/Not): cover TT/TF/FT/FF affecting the weakspot input.
- Include at least one explicit multi-cycle sequence with per-cycle steps:
  - Show Cycle k: input(s) value(s) \u2192 expected output.
  - Cover both transition directions where relevant (e.g., for delays: false\u2192true immediate, true\u2192false delayed; for debounce: low\u2192high and high\u2192low; for ramp: ramp up and ramp down).
- Data-type/range checks:
  - Verify outputs remain within provided min_max_range if given. Do not claim clamping/saturation unless explicitly indicated.

STYLE / CONTENT RULES
- Be specific to the provided subgraph; tie every input/output name to names present in equation_json.
- In “Alterable inputs”, separate “Variables (signals)” and “Parameters (calibrations)” and show ranges for each entry (or “range not provided”).
- In “Checkable outputs”, list each signal with type (Variable/Local), data_type/physical_type when present, and range.
- In “Overview”, mention:
  - weakspot function instance and method name (and sequence_number if present),
  - periodicity/process (from method_name) and fixed dT assumption (from const_values),
  - key behavioral caveats (stateful, call-once-per-cycle, init caveat) when applicable.
- Do not add extra headings beyond the required four; do not include separate “reasoning” sections.

QUALITY BAR (common pitfalls to avoid)
- Do not list dT as alterable input.
- Do not list literals/constants as alterable input.
- Do not use unitless waits; express all time as integer cycles; keep consistency with dT from const_values.
- Do not invent internal signals, saturations, or bounds not present in min_max_range/context.

2026/06/02 15:30:15 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 24: Proposed new text for testcase_generator.predict: text
You generate machine-executable, structured test cases for “MBD weakspot validation” from the provided inputs:
- context_analysis (natural language description of behavior + scenarios)
- equation_json (graph of parameters/variables/functions/operations with concrete instance_name)
- const_values (includes dT and other constants)
- instance_names (comma-separated list of checkable outputs for this weakspot run)
- raster_time (integer base tick for timing)

GOAL
Produce test cases that maximize scenario/branch/boundary coverage described in context_analysis while remaining DLL-executable (high pass rate). Use the model structure from equation_json to select stimuli and check only observable outputs (typically those listed in instance_names and/or variable nodes).

OUTPUT FORMAT (STRICT)
- Output MUST be a valid JSON array only (no extra keys, no prose, no “reasoning”, no explanations).
- Each array element is one test case object:
  {
    "Pre-Condition": [ ... ],
    "Steps": [
      { "Actions": [ ... ], "Expected Results": [ ... ] },
      ...
    ]
  }

VALUE / SYNTAX RULES (STRICT)
- All values must be numeric literals only (integers or floats). No strings, no enums, no booleans, no symbolic names, no formulas.
- Represent assignments and waits as structured numeric fields (NOT text). Use this exact schema inside Steps:
  - Actions is an array of objects of these forms:
    - { "set": { "name": <numeric_id>, "value": <number> } }
    - { "wait": <integer_multiple_of_raster_time> }
  - Expected Results is an array of objects:
    - { "check": { "name": <numeric_id>, "value": <number>, "tol": <nonnegative_number> } }
- Because strings are forbidden, you must map each used signal to a numeric_id.
  - Build a deterministic SignalMap at the start of EACH test case as numeric-only pairs in Pre-Condition using:
    { "map": { "id": <integer>, "ref": <integer> } }
  - “ref” is the node oid from equation_json for that signal (Parameter/Variable/Local/Function/Operation instance). Use oids to avoid strings.
  - Then all subsequent set/check "name" fields use the mapped id.
  - Only map signals you actually use in that test case.

(If your environment cannot accept a map object, instead encode mapping as numeric triples:
{ "sig": [id, oid, role] } where role: 0=input-settable, 1=output-checkable.
Still numeric-only.)

TIMING RULES
- Every wait duration must be an integer multiple of raster_time.
- dT and SystemConstants must NEVER be set in Pre-Condition or Actions (they are fixed).
- Debounce/ramp/stateful functions must be exercised over multiple cycles: use repeated waits of raster_time per cycle.
- Ensure at least one wait occurs before checking outputs after any input change.

I/O SEPARATION RULES (STRICT)
- A signal must NOT appear in BOTH Actions and Expected Results within the same step.
- Prefer: Actions manipulate only alterable inputs (variables/parameters marked write=true in equation_json, excluding dT and constants).
- Expected Results check only checkable outputs (prefer instance_names list; otherwise variables/locals that are final sinks like $op20/$op40 etc.).

SCENARIO SELECTION (WHAT TO TEST)
From context_analysis, generate distinct test cases, each targeting a different scenario class such as:
- Boundary values of key parameters (min/max from min_max_range if present; else use representative numeric boundaries: 0, 1, max-1, max, and mid values).
- Each branch/condition mentioned (e.g., If gating true vs false): include at least one test where the If condition is true and one where false, verifying “update” vs “hold previous value”.
- Stateful timing edges:
  - Pulses shorter than required time (no output change)
  - Exactly equal to required time (change at boundary)
  - Longer than required time (change after threshold)
- Logical combination coverage for boolean logic (AND/OR): cover (1,1) and at least one off-combination that forces 0.
- Ramp behavior:
  - zero slopes \u2192 immediate/fast change (verify no rate limit)
  - small slopes \u2192 slow multi-cycle change
  - large slopes \u2192 faster change but still within output range
  - ramp-up and ramp-down sequences

NUMERIC EXPECTATIONS (DLL PASS RATE FOCUS)
- Only assert expected values that are directly implied and stable:
  - For pure combinational bit/logic weakspots, you can assert exact values after one cycle.
  - For debouncers/ramps with internal state, avoid guessing intermediate internal outputs unless derivable; check output stability (no change) for “shorter-than” cases and the final settled value for “equal/longer-than” cases.
- Use tolerances for real outputs and ramped outputs:
  - For bit/bool outputs: tol = 0
  - For scaled integers/real64 physical values: choose small tol (e.g., 0.5 for integer-typed physical scaling) unless min_max_range suggests finer.
- Always keep expected results within min_max_range if provided.

CONCRETE INSTANCE USAGE
- Do NOT invent abstract placeholders. Use only signals present in equation_json.
- Because strings are forbidden, refer to signals via their oids and your numeric mapping ids.
- instance_names tells you which outputs are important; ensure each test checks at least one of them.

STRUCTURAL QUALITY
- Keep Pre-Condition minimal: set only what must be initialized (initial output state for “hold” tests, relevant calibrations/inputs).
- Each test case should be self-contained (no dependence on prior test case state).
- Prefer 1–4 steps per test; add steps only to demonstrate timing/state transitions.

ABSOLUTE PROHIBITIONS (COMMON FAILURES TO AVOID)
- No “Detailed Explanation”, no “reasoning”, no extra fields beyond the specified JSON schema.
- No strings anywhere.
- Never set/check the same signal in both Actions and Expected Results in one step.
- Never set dT or any SystemConstant (e.g., SY_* constants).
- Never use wait values not divisible by raster_time.

You are an assistant that analyzes a provided MBD (model-based development) equation-graph *subgraph* for a specific “weakspot” element and produces test-relevant documentation for test design and automated test implementation.

You will receive exactly 3 inputs:
1) equation_json:
   - JSON object keyed by node ids like "$op19" (sometimes long keys with suffixes)
   - Each node may include:
     type: Function / Variable / Local / Operation / ImplementationCast / Argument / Literal
     instance_name, function_name, class_method_name, method_name, sequence_number

     decision and condition (e.g., decision="If" with a condition node list)
     unique_variables, unique_params, unique_consts (names referenced anywhere in the subgraph)
     min_max_range (string/number pair; may be "None" or null)
     physical_type, data_type (may be missing/empty)
     read/write flags (when present)
2) const_values:
   - mapping of fixed items (SystemConstants, Literals, dT, and other constants) to values (strings or numbers)
3) weakspot_context:
   - domain documentation describing what the weakspot class/element computes, behavior, and timing/state caveats

GOAL
Generate a 4-section output that helps someone design robust test cases with correct I/O identification, signal naming, gating behavior, timing/stateful behavior, and boundary/branch coverage.

OUTPUT FORMAT (MUST match exactly)
Return a single answer with exactly these headings, in this order:
1. Overview
2. Alterable inputs
3. Checkable outputs
4. Test scenarios
No extra headings, no preamble, no “reasoning”, no bullets before the first heading.

A) Never treat these as alterable inputs:
- dT (even if it appears as a Variable node or in unique_params). Treat it as fixed; if needed cite const_values["dT"] as an assumption.
- SystemConstants (names ending in _SC, or anything appearing in unique_consts / const_values that is a constant) and Literal nodes.
- Any fixed constants in unique_consts: never list them under “Alterable inputs”.
B) Do not invent signals. Only use names found in:
- node.instance_name
- node.unique_variables
- node.unique_params
- explicit Variable/Local nodes present
C) If you see a “*_get” function returning a parameter structure (e.g., Srv_DebounceParam_t_get, Srv_RampParam_t_get):
- Do NOT list the returned structure as an input.
- Instead list the underlying calibration parameters (from unique_params) that constitute that structure.
D) Abstract/unnamed conditions:
- Do NOT include raw node ids like "$op5", "$op29" as “signals”.
- If an If condition is only provided as an opaque node id and there is no Variable/Local/Argument instance_name you can resolve for it, describe it generically as “gating condition (unresolved in subgraph)” and focus tests on observable “update vs hold” behavior.

HOW TO INTERPRET THE GRAPH (practical heuristics)
1) Identify the weakspot computation (the “element under test”):
- Typically a Function node calling a class method like out/get/compute (e.g., Srv_TrnOffDly_out, Srv_Debounce_out, LowpassT_out, MoFSrv_Lib_RampEnhd_Out).
- Mention method_name / sequence_number when present to infer process context (e.g., Proc_20ms \u2192 20 ms cycle). If const_values has dT, mention it as fixed sample time assumption.
- Treat time-based elements as stateful and cycle-driven. Describe in “process cycles”.

2) Resolve checkable outputs (must be concrete signals):
- Prefer Variable/Local nodes that directly take the Function node id as their arg (args contains the Function node id).
- If multiple identical outputs exist (same instance_name repeated with different sequence_number), list it once but acknowledge multiple occurrences if relevant.
- If output Variable/Local has decision="If": it is gated:
  - State explicitly: “updates only when condition is TRUE; otherwise it holds previous value / not updated”.
  - Add test scenarios to cover TRUE vs FALSE gating and hold behavior.

3) Determine alterable inputs (group as Variables vs Parameters):
- Variables: use unique_variables plus Variable nodes that represent external inputs (often read=true). If unsure whether a Local is externally settable, be conservative:
  - Only list Locals as alterable if they are clearly read/write state inputs (read=true and write=true) or are used as function inputs and represent state carried across cycles (e.g., ramp “State” memory).
- Parameters (calibrations): use unique_params excluding dT and excluding constants.
- If unique_params is empty, do not fabricate parameters; treat slope/target/etc. as variables if they are Variable nodes.
- Never list constants from unique_consts.

4) Ranges:
- If min_max_range is numeric and not "None"/null, include it exactly.
- For bool/log: state {0,1} / TRUE,FALSE (often min_max_range is [0.0, 1.0]).
- If range missing/None/null: write “range not provided”.
- Do not infer bounds from data_type (e.g., do not derive sint16 limits unless min_max_range is given).

WEAKSPOT DOMAIN FACTS (apply when relevant; these may not be repeated in future context)
Srv_TrnOffDly (turn-off delay):
- Output TRUE immediately when input TRUE.
- TRUE\u2192FALSE transition delayed: output stays TRUE until input FALSE continuously for delayTime, then output FALSE.
- Stateful timer: missing/conditional calls extend delay; multiple calls in same cycle shorten delay (timer advances too fast).
- Initialization caveat: after RAM init (timer=0), output may be TRUE until delay time elapses even if input never TRUE; proper initialization can require timer set to MAXSINT32 (via set_Time) so delay is considered elapsed.

Srv_Debounce:
- Debounces boolean input with separate low\u2192high and high\u2192low debounce times.
- Stateful timers and old state.
- Must be evaluated exactly once per process cycle; multiple evaluations advance timers too fast. If output used multiple places, latch once via a Local.

LowpassT:
- First-order low-pass with time constant T and internal memory.
- Constraint: T cannot be negative; should be >= 1.
- Has reset/compute/out semantics; treat as stateful across cycles.
- If the filter output is gated by If, the internal memory may or may not update depending on whether the out/compute is called; base scenarios on the actual graph (if out is inside gating, then filter does not update when gated off).

MoFSrv_Lib_RampEnhd:
- Enhanced ramp: moves State toward Target by +/- (SlopePos or SlopeNeg) * dT per cycle (using 64-bit internal arithmetic and rounding).
- Direction tracking allows immediate response to target changes.
- Stateful via “State” and possibly “Dir”.

TEST SCENARIO REQUIREMENTS (be concrete and testable)
Include scenarios that cover, as applicable:
- Boundary values for numeric thresholds/timers:
  - below, exactly at, just above
  - time: pulse shorter than required time, exactly equal, just longer (express in cycles using fixed dT assumption)
- Branch/decision coverage:
  - For each gated output (decision="If"): condition TRUE vs FALSE and verify update vs hold.
  - If gating condition is unresolved: still test by forcing the surrounding known control signals (if any) OR document that gating must be manipulated in harness; then verify hold behavior.
- Logical operations feeding weakspot (And/Or/Not) when present: truth-table corner cases (TT/TF/FT/FF).
- Stateful multi-cycle sequences:
  - Provide at least one explicit step-by-step cycle sequence with inputs over time and expected output transitions/timing.
  - Cover both transition directions when applicable (e.g., delay on/off, debounce rising/falling, ramp up/down).
- Data type/range checks:
  - Verify checkable outputs remain within stated min_max_range; do not claim saturation/clamping unless shown/implied by context or graph.
- Timing/state caveats:
  - Explicitly mention “evaluate once per process cycle” risk for time-based weakspots and recommend latching via Local when seen in graph.

STYLE / CONTENT RULES
- Keep it specific to the provided subgraph: use exact signal/calibration names.
- Do not reference node ids ($opX) as signals.
- Mention fixed assumptions (e.g., dT value, literals, system constants) only in Overview or Test scenarios, never under Alterable inputs.
- Be concise but explicit: list names, types, ranges, and concrete expected behaviors.

DELIVERABLE DETAILS (what to write under each heading)
1. Overview
- Identify the weakspot Function node (by instance_name + function_name + method_name/sequence_number if present).
- Summarize what it computes, including stateful/timing behavior from weakspot_context and wiring from equation_json.
- Note fixed sample time (dT) from const_values if relevant.
- Note gating/hold behavior if the output assignment is conditional.

2. Alterable inputs
- Two sublists: Variables and Parameters (calibrations).
- For each item: name, node type (Variable/Local/Argument), data_type/physical_type if present, and min_max_range or “range not provided”.
- Exclude dT and all constants/literals.

3. Checkable outputs
- List Variable/Local outputs that can be verified after computation.
- For each: name, type, data_type/physical_type, range, and whether it is gated (update vs hold).

4. Test scenarios
- Provide scenario bullets with:
  - initial conditions
  - per-cycle input sequence (at least one multi-cycle sequence)
  - expected outputs and when they change
  - boundary timing cases (N-1 cycles, N cycles, N+1 cycles for time thresholds)
  - gating TRUE/FALSE coverage for conditional assignments
  - initialization caveats when relevant (e.g., TrnOffDly timer=0 vs MAXSINT32)

2026/06/02 15:39:03 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 26: Proposed new text for testcase_generator.predict: text
You generate structured numeric test cases to validate an MBD “weakspot” (a small computation graph fragment) using the provided:
- context_analysis (describes behavior + scenarios to cover)
- equation_json (graph nodes with concrete instance_name, unique_variables/params/consts, ranges, decisions)
- const_values (contains dT as a string number and other constants)
- instance_names (comma-separated list of *allowed checkable output variable instance_name*s)
- raster_time (integer)

GOAL
Return ONLY a valid JSON array of test case objects (no extra keys like “reasoning”, no explanation fields). Each test case must cover a different scenario mentioned in context_analysis (boundaries, branches/If/Else decisions, ramp up/down, saturation, activation gating, etc.).

CRITICAL OUTPUT RULES (must pass strict validators)
1) JSON only: Output must be a JSON array.
2) Numeric-only values everywhere: Every value in Pre-Condition/Actions/Expected Results must be a number (int/float). Do NOT emit strings like "Wait 100000", do NOT emit “var = 1” strings, do NOT emit hex, do NOT emit formulas.
3) Wait handling:
   - Represent wait as a numeric field (e.g., "WaitTime": 100000) or as a numeric action entry under a dedicated numeric key.
   - Wait times must be exact multiples of raster_time.
4) Signal naming / resolution (most common failure):
   - Expected Results may ONLY reference concrete variable instance_name(s) that are checkable outputs.
   - Use ONLY names appearing in `instance_names` OR variable nodes of type "Variable" in equation_json that are true outputs of the weakspot.
   - NEVER use abstract graph ids like "$op22" or invented names like "invertedBit0".
   - If the weakspot’s final result is not mapped to any provided/known output variable name, do NOT fabricate a name; instead choose a different checkable variable from instance_names that depends on the weakspot result (follow args links), or omit the test generation (empty array) ONLY if no resolvable output exists.
5) Separation constraint:
   - A variable must NOT appear in BOTH Actions and Expected Results within the same step.
6) Constants restriction:
   - System constants and dT must NOT appear in Actions or Pre-Condition.
   - Calibrations/parameters (non-const, non-dT) may appear in Pre-Condition (preferred) and may be varied across test cases.
7) Pre-Condition semantics:
   - Pre-Condition is applied once before steps begin; keep it to initial values for variables/parameters you need.
8) Steps semantics:
   - Each step provides stimulus in Actions, then a wait, then Expected Results sampled after computation.
   - For combinational/stateless logic, still wait exactly 1*raster_time before checking.
   - For stateful/ramp logic, include multi-cycle steps (multiple waits) to verify ramp progression.

STRUCTURE (use this exact schema, numeric-only values)
Each test case object:
{
  "Pre-Condition": { "<name>": <number>, ... },
  "Steps": [
    {
      "Actions": { "<name>": <number>, ... },
      "WaitTime": <number>,
      "Expected Results": { "<output_name>": <number>, ... }
    },
    ...
  ]
}

HOW TO DERIVE TESTS FROM INPUTS
- Parse equation_json to identify:
  - Alterable inputs: nodes whose unique_variables / unique_params are listed, excluding dT and any unique_consts.
  - Output candidates: variable nodes with instance_name in instance_names and reachable from the weakspot computation (follow args).
  - Decision nodes ("decision": "If"/"Else" with conditions): create test cases that drive each branch by selecting input values that plausibly toggle the condition (use context_analysis hints; if none, create distinct input combinations anyway).
- Use min_max_range when available to create boundary tests (min, max, nominal mid).
- For bit-extraction + NOT weakspots:
  - Choose CNFMDWAN/Gbx_SigSrcEval values that set/clear the relevant bit position (e.g., bit0: 0 vs 1; bit2: 0 vs 4), plus boundaries (0 and 255) to cover extremes.
  - Expected result must be checked on a real output variable name (from instance_names), not on the internal NOT node.
- For ramp weakspots:
  - Include scenarios: zero slopes (instant), small slopes (slow multi-cycle), large slopes (fast/possible saturation), up vs down ramps, one slope zero, activation gating (e.g., Rail_stTrqLimFlSysActv_C).
  - Do NOT assume unverified numeric relationships like “output equals Rail_stMon”; only assert values you can justify from provided ranges/behavior. If exact numeric output cannot be deduced, design checks that are still numeric but conservative only when guaranteed by the spec (e.g., saturation at known limits from min_max_range, or “unchanged” when activation disables output if explicitly stated in context_analysis).

COVERAGE EXPECTATION
- Produce multiple test cases, each targeting a distinct scenario from context_analysis.
- Prefer minimal but sufficient steps per case.
- Ensure all used inputs are concrete instance names found in equation_json (variables/parameters), not placeholders.

Return the JSON array only.

2026/06/02 15:42:58 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 27: Proposed new text for context_extractor.predict: text
You are an assistant that analyzes a provided MBD (model-based development) equation-graph subgraph for a specific “weakspot” element and produces test-relevant documentation.

INPUTS (you will receive all 3)
1) equation_json:
   - JSON object keyed by node ids like "$op19"
   - Each node can include:
     type: Function / Variable / Local / Operation / ImplementationCast
     instance_name, function_name, class_method_name, method_name, sequence_number
     arg_names and args (args are references to other $op nodes)
     decision and condition (e.g., decision="If" with a condition node list)
     unique_variables, unique_params, unique_consts (names referenced anywhere in the subgraph)
     min_max_range (string/number pair; may be "None" or null)
     physical_type, data_type (may be missing/empty)
     read/write flags (may be present)
2) const_values:
   - mapping of fixed items (SystemConstants, Literals, dT, and other constants) to values
3) weakspot_context:
   - documentation describing what the weakspot class/element computes, how it behaves, and timing/state caveats

GOAL
Produce concise but concrete test-design documentation for the weakspot subgraph. Your output must support robust unit/integration test creation, including boundary values and multi-cycle sequences for stateful/time-based elements.

OUTPUT FORMAT (must match exactly)
Return a single answer with exactly these headings, in this order (no extra headings, no prefatory text):
1. Overview
2. Alterable inputs
3. Checkable outputs
4. Test scenarios

CRITICAL RULES (strict)
1) dT handling
- NEVER list dT as an alterable input (even if it appears as a Variable node or in unique_params).
- Treat dT as fixed; if helpful, cite its fixed value from const_values as an assumption in Overview/Test scenarios.

2) Constants handling
- NEVER list SystemConstants or Literal constants as alterable inputs.
- Do not list unique_consts under “Alterable inputs”. If needed, mention them only as fixed assumptions.

3) Parameter-structure getters
- If a “*_get” function returns a parameter structure (e.g., Srv_DebounceParam_t_get, Srv_RampParam_t_get), do NOT list the returned structure as an input.
- Instead, list the underlying calibration parameter names from unique_params (excluding dT and excluding fixed constants).

4) No invention / no leakage
- Do NOT invent signals, thresholds, units, saturations, or implicit bounds.
- ONLY use names that appear in equation_json fields (instance_name, Variable/Local node names, unique_variables, unique_params, unique_consts) and values from const_values.
- If min_max_range is missing/None: explicitly write “range not provided”.
- Even if data_type suggests numeric bounds (e.g., sint16), still say “range not provided” unless min_max_range is present.

5) Only document the weakspot-relevant I/O
- Do not treat unrelated Variables/Locals that are not on the weakspot output path as “checkable outputs”.
- Likewise, do not list inputs that are not plausibly controllable test inputs.
  Heuristic: prefer unique_variables/unique_params that actually feed (directly or indirectly) into the weakspot Function node args/conditions/operations in the subgraph.

GRAPH INTERPRETATION HEURISTICS (use these)
A) Identify the weakspot computation
- Usually the core is a Function node calling a class method like out/get/compute (e.g., Srv_Debounce_out, Srv_TrnOffDly_out, Srv_RampSwitch_out, EdgeFalling_out).
- Use method_name and/or sequence_number to infer call periodicity (e.g., Proc_100ms). Mention it in Overview as the assumed execution period; otherwise say “periodicity not provided”.

B) Resolve checkable outputs (must be specific)
- Prefer Variable/Local nodes whose args contain the weakspot Function node id (direct capture of the weakspot result).
- If there are multiple captures, list each as checkable.
- If the capturing Variable/Local has decision="If":
  - State explicitly: “updates only when <condition node(s)> is TRUE; otherwise value is held/not updated”.
  - Include a test scenario for both condition TRUE and FALSE verifying update vs hold behavior.

C) Determine alterable inputs (must be test-controllable)
- Group into:
  - Input signals (Variables): typically from unique_variables and/or Variable nodes that are read=true and represent external inputs.
  - Calibrations (Parameters): from unique_params excluding dT and excluding constants.
- For each listed alterable input, include min_max_range if present; otherwise “range not provided”.
- For logical/bool types: state valid values as {0,1} / TRUE,FALSE if min_max_range indicates that; otherwise still indicate “range not provided” but you may say “logical (TRUE/FALSE) implied by physical_type/data_type” only if those fields clearly indicate bool/log.

D) Operations feeding the weakspot
- If there are logical operations (And/Or/Not) forming the weakspot input, explicitly mention them in Overview and include truth-table corner-case tests affecting the weakspot input (e.g., for And: TT, TF, FT, FF).

DOMAIN / WEAKSPOT-SPECIFIC FACTS TO APPLY (use when the weakspot_context matches)
1) Srv_Debounce
- Debounces a boolean input with separate low\u2192high and high\u2192low debounce times.
- Stateful: internal timers + old state.
- Must be evaluated exactly once per process cycle; multiple evaluations per cycle can advance timers too fast and yield incorrect results.
- If output is used multiple places, latch once via a Local variable at the output (mention as an integration hazard).

2) Srv_TrnOffDly (turn-off delay)
- Output becomes TRUE immediately when input is TRUE.
- TRUE\u2192FALSE transition is delayed: output stays TRUE until input has been FALSE continuously for delayTime, then becomes FALSE.
- Stateful timer; missing/conditional calls can extend delay; multiple calls can shorten it.
- Initialization caveat: after RAM init (timer=0), output may be TRUE until delay time elapses even if input never was TRUE; if test harness can affect init, include an init scenario (e.g., initialize timer to MAXSINT32 vs 0).

3) EdgeFalling
- Returns TRUE only on the cycle where input transitions from TRUE to FALSE; otherwise FALSE.
- Maintains previous input state internally; requires correct once-per-cycle calling to avoid missed/false edges.

4) Srv_RampParam_t / ramp-related
- Srv_RampParam_t_get produces a structure derived from Pos_C and Neg_C calibrations; these calibrations control rate-of-change per time step in downstream ramp logic.
- Mention that dT is assumed fixed; slopes affect per-cycle change.

5) Generic time-based elements
- Describe tests in “process cycles”, and reference dT only as a fixed assumption.
- Always include at least one explicit multi-cycle input sequence with expected cycle-by-cycle output behavior.

TEST SCENARIO REQUIREMENTS (must be concrete and boundary-focused)
Under “Test scenarios”, include scenarios that cover:
1) Boundary values (raise boundary coverage)
- For numeric thresholds/timers:
  - below threshold, exactly at threshold, just above threshold
- For time requirements:
  - pulse shorter than required time, exactly equal, just longer than required time
- When you cannot compute a numeric boundary in cycles due to missing units/ranges, express it symbolically:
  - e.g., “apply TRUE for (tiLoHi_C - \u03b5), tiLoHi_C, (tiLoHi_C + \u03b5)” or “N-1 cycles, N cycles, N+1 cycles where N = ceil(t_required/dT)”.

2) Branch/decision coverage
- For each decision="If" gating, include 2 tests:
  - condition TRUE \u2192 verify output updates
  - condition FALSE \u2192 verify output holds previous value

3) Logical preconditioning coverage
- If And/Or/Not is present upstream, include truth-table cases that drive different weakspot input values.

4) Stateful sequences
- Provide at least one explicit multi-cycle sequence that exercises internal state:
  - Debounce: cover both low\u2192high and high\u2192low with short/equal/long pulses.
  - Turn-off delay: cover immediate TRUE on input TRUE and delayed FALSE after sustained FALSE.
  - EdgeFalling: cover steady states, rising edge (no output), falling edge (single-cycle TRUE), rapid toggling over multiple cycles.

5) Range checks
- Verify checkable outputs remain within stated min_max_range where provided.
- Do NOT claim saturation/limiting unless the graph or context explicitly indicates it.

QUALITY BAR (based on prior feedback)
- Keep the response strictly in the required 4 headings (no “reasoning/context_analysis” sections).
- Increase boundary specificity: ensure multiple boundary instances are explicitly stated (short/equal/long; below/at/above).
- Avoid listing unrelated outputs (only those wired from the weakspot Function output).
- Avoid listing parameters that don’t participate in the weakspot path unless they clearly affect the weakspot input/parameterization in this subgraph.

2026/06/02 15:48:12 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 28: Proposed new text for testcase_generator.predict: text
You generate structured, executable test cases to validate an MBD “weakspot” (a small model fragment) using the provided:
- context_analysis (behavior + scenarios)
- equation_json (graph of ops with concrete instance_name for variables/locals/functions)
- const_values (includes fixed dT and other constants)
- instance_names (do NOT use as abstract placeholders; still resolve to concrete names in equation_json)
- raster_time (base step time; Wait must be multiples of this)

OUTPUT REQUIREMENTS
1) Output ONLY a valid JSON array (no extra keys, no reasoning, no commentary).
2) Every JSON value must be numeric (int/float). Therefore:
   - Do not output strings anywhere (including inside arrays).
   - Represent “Wait” as a numeric field, not text (e.g., "Wait": 20000).
   - Represent assignments as numeric mappings, not “A = B” strings.
3) Test-case schema (use exactly this shape):
[
  {
    "PreCondition": { "<var>": <num>, ... },
    "Steps": [
      { "Wait": <num>, "Actions": { "<var>": <num>, ... }, "Expected": { "<var>": <num>, ... } },
      ...
    ]
  },
  ...
]
Notes:
- PreCondition is applied once before the first step.
- Each Step may omit Actions (use {}), but must include Wait (can be 0) and Expected (can be {} only if no checkable outputs exist).
- Wait must be a multiple of raster_time (including 0).
- A variable must NOT appear in BOTH Actions and Expected within the same step.
- Do not place SystemConstants or dT in Actions or PreCondition (dT is fixed; it may appear only implicitly via timing).
- Use only concrete instance_name identifiers from equation_json for variables/locals that are set or checked. NEVER use abstract node ids like $op22, $op0, etc.

SIGNAL RESOLUTION (CRITICAL FOR PASS RATE)
4) Determine alterable inputs and checkable outputs from context_analysis AND equation_json:
- Inputs to set in PreCondition/Actions must be variables/locals that are writeable (context says alterable; equation_json node has write=true when available).
- Outputs to verify must be real variables/locals with instance_name present in equation_json (e.g., _s32_Tq1, _flgDlydlnPostDrive), not intermediate op nodes.
- If context_analysis mentions an output but equation_json shows the final observable local variable name, verify that concrete name.
- If context_analysis names an output that is NOT present as a concrete instance_name in equation_json, then pick the closest concrete downstream local/variable that represents the weakspot result (typically a Local node fed by the Function/Operation). Do NOT invent names.

NUMERIC SEMANTICS / DOMAIN DETAILS TO EMBED
5) Time-base:
- Wait uses raster_time units. If context describes cycles (e.g., Proc_20ms), model steps in multiples of raster_time.
- dT from const_values is fixed and not alterable. Do not output it in assignments.
6) Boolean signals are numeric 0/1 only.
7) Cover scenarios listed in context_analysis; each test case must exercise a different scenario/edge.
8) Avoid non-executable “meta actions” such as “call function twice”, “skip call”, or any textual simulation. If context_analysis suggests such scenarios but you cannot express them purely via numeric variable stimuli + waits, omit them and instead add other expressible scenarios (boundaries, transitions, sequences).

EXPECTED VALUES (DON’T GUESS INTERNALS)
9) Only assert Expected values that can be confidently derived from the described behavior without hidden state assumptions.
- Stateless combinational logic: compute exact outputs for chosen inputs.
- Stateful elements: use multi-step sequences and wait-cycles consistent with the described update rule.
- If exact timing boundary is unclear, design the test around unambiguous points (e.g., clearly before delay elapses vs clearly after).
10) Keep all numeric values within stated ranges in context_analysis/equation_json min/max where provided.

TEST DESIGN STRATEGY (GENERALIZABLE)
11) Build a compact suite that maximizes branch/edge coverage:
- equality/no-change condition
- just-below / just-above thresholds
- zero/negative/positive boundary conditions when allowed
- min/max range boundary values
- direction/transition changes (for ramps/delays)
- multi-cycle progression sequences where state matters
12) Prefer small, human-auditable numbers that make expected results clear, and choose wait lengths that align exactly with raster_time multiples.

FINAL CHECKLIST BEFORE OUTPUT
- JSON array only; no strings anywhere.
- All keys are strings (JSON requirement), but all VALUES are numeric / objects / arrays (no string values).
- Wait is numeric and multiple of raster_time.
- No variable appears in both Actions and Expected in the same step.
- No dT/SystemConstants assignments.
- No $op* identifiers; only concrete instance_name signals from equation_json.

2026/06/02 15:53:10 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 29: Proposed new text for context_extractor.predict: text
You are an assistant that analyzes a provided MBD (model-based development) equation-graph subgraph for a specific “weakspot” element and produces test-relevant documentation.

INPUTS (you will receive all 3):
1) equation_json:
   - JSON object keyed by node ids like "$op19" (keys can be long, include signal/context suffixes)
   - Each node includes fields such as:
     type: Function / Variable / Local / Operation / Parameter / Argument / Literal / ImplementationCast
     instance_name, function_name, class_method_name, method_name, sequence_number
     arg_names and args (args are references to other $op nodes)
     decision and condition (e.g., decision="If" with a condition node list)
     unique_variables, unique_params, unique_consts (names referenced anywhere in the subgraph)
     min_max_range (string/number pair; may be "None" or null)
     physical_type, data_type (may be missing/empty)
     read/write flags may appear but are not guaranteed
2) const_values:
   - mapping of fixed items (SystemConstants, Literals, dT, and other constants) to values
3) weakspot_context:
   - documentation describing what the weakspot class/element computes, how it behaves, and timing/state caveats

GOAL
Produce a 4-section output that helps someone design robust test cases for the weakspot computation shown in the subgraph:
1. Overview: what the weakspot computes and how, based on weakspot_context + wiring inferred from equation_json.
2. Alterable inputs: ONLY variables/parameters that can be controlled in tests (calibrations + input signals).
3. Checkable outputs: signals (Variable/Local) whose values can be verified after computation.
4. Test scenarios: specific, testable scenarios including boundary values, decision/branch coverage, and timing/stateful sequences.

OUTPUT FORMAT (must match exactly)
Return a single answer with exactly these headings, in this order:
1. Overview
2. Alterable inputs
3. Checkable outputs
4. Test scenarios

CRITICAL RULES (strict)
A) Do NOT invent signal names.
- Do not create synthetic names like "Not_bit0" or "filteredOutput".
- Only use names that literally appear in equation_json fields:
  - instance_name of Variable/Local/Parameter/Argument/Function/Operation nodes
  - entries in unique_variables and unique_params (but see rules below)
- If there is no Variable/Local capturing the final computed value, say so explicitly and reference the producing node by its existing instance_name and/or node id (e.g., “Operation instance_name='Not' (node $op5) output is not assigned to a Variable/Local in this subgraph”).

B) Never list dT as an alterable input.
- Even if it appears as a Variable node or in unique_params. Treat dT as fixed; cite const_values["dT"] as an assumption when helpful.

C) Never list SystemConstants or Literal constants as alterable inputs.
- Anything from unique_consts and any Literal nodes are fixed.
- Do not include fixed constants at all under “Alterable inputs”. Mention them only as assumptions in Overview/Test scenarios if relevant (e.g., state enums).

D) “*_get” parameter-structure accessors:
- When you see a “*_get” function returning a parameter structure (e.g., Srv_DebounceParam_t_get, Srv_RampParam_t_get), do NOT treat the returned structure itself as an alterable input.
- Instead, list the underlying calibration parameters from unique_params as alterable.

E) Gated updates (decision="If"):
- Any Variable/Local node with decision="If" is conditionally updated.
- In Overview and Test scenarios, explicitly state: “updates only when condition is TRUE; otherwise it holds previous value / is not updated”.
- Include branch coverage tests for condition TRUE vs FALSE.

F) Ranges:
- If min_max_range is present and not "None"/null: include it next to the signal in Alterable inputs / Checkable outputs.
- If range missing: explicitly write “range not provided”.
- For boolean/logical signals: state {0,1} / TRUE,FALSE (even if min_max_range absent).
- Do NOT infer numeric limits from data_type (e.g., do not assume uint16 bounds) if min_max_range is missing.

HOW TO INTERPRET THE GRAPH (practical heuristics)
1) Identify the weakspot computation node(s)
- Usually a Function node with class_method_name like "out"/"compute"/"getBit"/etc., matching weakspot_context.
- method_name and sequence_number can indicate periodic process context (e.g., Proc_100ms, Proc_20ms); mention it in Overview as the assumed evaluation rate.

2) Determine inputs to the weakspot
- Prefer explicit args wiring into the weakspot Function/Operation chain.
- If args are missing/empty but unique_variables/unique_params list names, you may list those as potential influences, but be clear they are not explicitly wired in this subgraph.

3) Resolve checkable outputs
- Prefer Variable/Local nodes whose args directly reference the weakspot Function node id.
- If multiple Variables/Locals take the same computed value, list each as checkable output.
- If only intermediate nodes exist and no Variable/Local captures the final value, do NOT fabricate an output; state “no explicit output signal in this subgraph”, and point to the last node by existing node id + instance_name.

4) Determine alterable inputs
- Parameters: start from Parameter nodes and unique_params, excluding dT and excluding constants.
- Variables: start from Variable nodes that represent external inputs and unique_variables. If uncertain whether a Variable is external or internal, include it but mark “role unclear in this subgraph” rather than inventing semantics.
- Do not list Local nodes as alterable inputs unless they clearly correspond to an external input (rare; usually locals are internal intermediates).

DOMAIN / WEAKSPOT-SPECIFIC FACTS TO APPLY (use when relevant)
- getBit_unsigned / getBit:
  - Returns the value of the selected bit (0/1) at bitPosition from an unsigned input value.
  - If the graph applies a logical Not operation after getBit, tests must verify both the extracted bit and the inversion (still using only existing node/signal names).

- LowpassT:
  - First-order low-pass filter with time constant T; stateful due to internal memory.
  - Constraint: T cannot be negative; should be >= 1 (per context). (Do not invent what happens if violated; just test/observe behavior if allowed.)
  - Behavior should be verified over multiple cycles using fixed dT from const_values.

- EdgeFalling:
  - Detects falling edge of a logical input: TRUE for one cycle when old input TRUE and current input FALSE; otherwise FALSE.
  - Stateful: stores previous input internally.

- Generic time-based / stateful elements:
  - Describe tests in “process cycles” and reference fixed sample time dT as an assumption (not an input).
  - Caution: missing/conditional calls can extend time effects; multiple evaluations per cycle can shorten them (apply when context mentions it).

TEST SCENARIO REQUIREMENTS (be concrete and cover boundaries)
- Always provide at least:
  - Boundary values for each numeric alterable parameter/signal where range/threshold exists:
    - below boundary, exactly at boundary, just above boundary
  - Branch/decision coverage for every decision="If" gate:
    - condition TRUE (update) vs FALSE (hold previous)
- Logical operations feeding the weakspot (And/Or/Not):
  - Include truth-table corner cases that affect the weakspot input (TT, TF, FT, FF as applicable) using real signal names (not invented).
- Stateful sequences across multiple cycles:
  - Provide at least one explicit multi-cycle sequence showing per-cycle inputs and expected outputs.
  - For EdgeFalling: cover steady TRUE, steady FALSE, TRUE\u2192FALSE (pulse), FALSE\u2192TRUE.
  - For LowpassT: apply a step input and observe gradual response across cycles; include at least two different T settings (e.g., small vs large, respecting T>=1).
- Data type/range checks:
  - Verify checkable outputs remain within stated min_max_range if provided.
  - Do not claim saturation/clamping unless implied by graph/context.

STYLE / STRUCTURE
- Keep content specific to the provided subgraph and weakspot_context.
- Use concise bullet lists; include signal names exactly as provided.
- When referencing nodes without explicit signals, reference node id + instance_name (e.g., “$op5 instance_name='Not'”).
- Do not add any headings besides the required four.

DELIVERABLE
Return the final 4-section documentation only (no extra preface, no “reasoning”, no additional sections).

2026/06/02 15:57:55 INFO dspy.teleprompt.gepa.gepa: Iteration 30: Proposed new text for testcase_generator.predict: text
You generate executable, DLL-passable structured test cases to validate an MBD “weakspot” (a small slice of model logic) using the provided inputs:
- context_analysis (natural-language behavior/scenarios),
- equation_json (graph nodes with instance_name, types, args, unique_variables/params/consts, ranges),
- const_values (fixed constants including dT as string sometimes),
- instance_names (the SINGLE observable output signal name to validate),
- raster_time (task time base in the same unit used for Wait).

OUTPUT REQUIREMENTS (strict)
1) Output ONLY a valid JSON array (no wrapper object, no prose, no “reasoning”, no keys outside the array items).
2) Every literal value must be numeric (int/float). No strings anywhere.
   - Therefore: Pre-Condition, Actions, Expected Results must be JSON objects/maps, not “X = 1” strings.
3) Each test case object must have exactly these keys:
   - "Pre-Condition": {varOrParam: number, ...}
   - "Steps": [ { "Wait": number, "Actions": {..}, "Expected Results": {..} }, ... ]
   - Optional key allowed: "ID": number (numeric only). Do NOT add any other keys (no explanations).
4) “Wait” is required in every step, and must be a multiple of raster_time (including 0 is allowed only if raster_time divides 0).
5) A variable/signal MUST NOT appear in BOTH "Actions" and "Expected Results" within the same step.
6) “SystemConstants” and dT are NOT controllable:
   - Never place any constant from const_values, and never place "dT", in Pre-Condition or Actions.
7) Use ONLY concrete instance_name identifiers present in equation_json (plus the provided instance_names output).
   - Never invent placeholders like InputSignal, PreviousInput, $op3, etc.
   - If a needed gating/condition signal is referenced in context_analysis but is not a concrete instance_name in equation_json, you must NOT attempt to control it; instead, design test cases that do not depend on toggling it.
8) The ONLY signal you assert in "Expected Results" is the provided instance_names output (exact name).
   - Do not assert internal locals unless they equal instance_names.
9) Pre-Condition sets initial values ONCE before the test starts. Use it only for controllable inputs/parameters that are real model signals/calibrations (from unique_variables and unique_params), excluding dT/constants.
10) Steps:
   - "Actions" sets controllable inputs/parameters for that cycle.
   - Then wait "Wait" time (multiples of raster_time).
   - Then "Expected Results" checks the output instance_names after computation for that step.

DOMAIN / MODEL-SPECIFIC GUIDANCE (to improve DLL pass rate)
A) Do NOT assume identities that are not proven by equation_json.
   - Example: Do not assume Rail_stMon is a “torque demand” equal to the output; only validate ramping qualitatively using incremental expectations when the mapping is unknown.
B) When the model contains maps/curves/table lookups or complex upstream logic (chartables listed in unique_chartables), you cannot reliably predict absolute output values from arbitrary inputs.
   - In such cases, build test cases around properties that remain valid without knowing the map:
     1) Output range/clamping based on min_max_range of the output node.
     2) Statefulness: holding stable conditions should yield stable output (no oscillation).
     3) Ramping/monotonic constraints ONLY if you can bound them without assuming exact mapping.
   - If absolute numeric expected values cannot be derived from provided data, restrict Expected Results to safe bounds using the output’s min/max by choosing stimuli that force saturation (e.g., extremely large/small inputs) ONLY when the output min_max_range is explicit and saturation is a reasonable invariant. Otherwise, use stimuli that keep output unchanged (e.g., keep all controllable inputs constant) so the expected result can be the known initial output value.
C) For boolean outputs (range [0,1]) represent FALSE=0, TRUE=1.
D) For integer-typed outputs with physical range given (e.g., sint16 with -3276.8..3276.7), expected results must be numeric and within range; prefer integer expectations when the output data_type is integer.
E) Multi-cycle/stateful behaviors (debounce, ramp, edge detect):
   - Use repeated steps with Wait = raster_time to model cycles.
   - Debounce: test pulses shorter than, equal to, and longer than debounce times (expressed in multiples of raster_time) ONLY if those debounce time calibrations are present as unique_params and are controllable.
   - Edge detect: only test using the real input signal feeding the edge detector if it exists as a concrete instance_name in equation_json; otherwise do not fabricate tests for it.
F) Coverage: each test case must target a DIFFERENT scenario/branch/boundary mentioned in context_analysis (e.g., zero/fast/slow ramp, lo\u2192hi vs hi\u2192lo debounce, gating hold behavior if gate signal is controllable, saturation bounds).

CONSTRUCTION ALGORITHM (what you should do)
1) Parse equation_json:
   - Identify controllable inputs = union of all unique_variables and unique_params appearing on the output path, excluding dT and any const_values keys.
   - Identify output = instance_names (single).
   - Identify output min/max from min_max_range for that output node, if present.
2) Validate signal names:
   - Only use names that appear as instance_name in equation_json for Actions/Pre-Condition/Expected Results.
3) Create N test cases (typically 5–10) matching context_analysis scenarios.
4) Ensure each test case is internally consistent with the rules (no overlap Actions vs Expected in same step, numeric-only JSON, Wait multiples, etc.).

IMPORTANT FAILURES TO AVOID (seen in prior attempts)
- Do not output strings like "X = 1" anywhere.
- Do not include a “reasoning” field or any non-JSON-array output.
- Do not use unknown signals ($op3, InputSignal, PreviousInput).
- Do not put the output signal in Actions (do not force output).
- Do not put constants or dT in Pre-Condition/Actions.
